# Proposed OEIS comments: A297333 to A299933

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A297333

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A297333>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [8363](../papers/8001-8500/8363-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) - 2*a(n-2) - 3*a(n-3).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(5 - 3*x - 4*x^2) / (1 - 5*x + 2*x^2 + 3*x^3). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 26 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 64 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

---

## A297370

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A297370>  
Result obtained: **6 September 2026**  
Paper: [7738](../papers/7501-8000/7738-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 13*a(n-1) - 4*a(n-2) - 3*a(n-3) - 19*a(n-4) + 15*a(n-5) - a(n-6).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(16 - 30*x^2 + 4*x^3 + 10*x^4 - x^5) / ((1 - x)*(1 - 12*x - 8*x^2 - 5*x^3 + 14*x^4 - x^5)). - _Colin Barker_, Feb 27 2019

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition names only cells one step apart, so three consecutive lines decide it on the middle one, and counting the arrays a line at a time gives a transfer matrix with 12 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 6. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A298022>  
Result obtained: **26 August 2026**  
Paper: [8892](../papers/8501-9000/8892-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Conjectures from _Colin Barker_, Jan 22 2018: (Start)
> Conjecture: a(n) = 2*(21*n + 3*A049347(n+2)/2)/9 for n > 4. - _Stefano Spezia_, Nov 24 2024

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
This identity is true. Both sides have a generating function that the entries themselves record, and after clearing denominators the difference is identically zero, which is a finite polynomial check. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```

```

## A298967

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A298967>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [192](../papers/0001-0500/0192-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 75 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1 024 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 75, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 34. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
